```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/crates/actor-x1/notes/lifecycle.md",
  "content": "# RuntimeZC lifecycle (0.6.0)\n\nReference for the zerocopy runtime's lifecycle API\n([`crate::runtime_zc::Runtime`] +\n[`crate::runtime_zc::Handle`]). The module rustdoc\n([`runtime_zc`]) carries a runnable worked example; this\nfile carries the design rationale.\n\n## Surface\n\n- `pub trait Runtime<S: BufRefStore>` — `startup` (returns a\n  `Handle<S>`), `pool` (borrows the underlying [`Pool<S>`]).\n- `pub struct RuntimeZC<S>` — implements `Runtime<S>`; built\n  via the inherent constructor `RuntimeZC::new(pool)`.\n- `pub struct Handle<S>` — owned by the caller during the\n  run; carries per-actor senders + join handles + a pool\n  clone. Methods:\n  - `run(duration)` — sleep a run window while actor threads\n    process messages.\n  - `reset_count()` — zero every actor's per-thread count.\n  - `query_count() -> u64` — read the aggregate without\n    stopping.\n  - `pool() -> &Pool<S>` — accessor for post-startup\n    `get_msg` / inspection.\n  - `stop() -> u64` — graceful shutdown + join + aggregate.\n  - `Drop` — silent graceful shutdown if `stop` was not\n    called.\n\n## Typical usage shape\n\n- **Smoke / bin** (`goalzc`): one `startup`, one warmup\n  `run`, `reset_count`, one measurement `run`, `stop`. The\n  reported throughput excludes warmup. See\n  `crates/actor-x1/src/bin/goalzc.rs`.\n- **Amortized bench** (`goalzc-crit`): one `startup` outside\n  `b.iter_custom`, one warmup `run` outside the loop, then\n  per criterion sample `reset_count` → `run(measurement)` →\n  `query_count`. `stop` happens implicitly on `Drop` at the\n  end of the bench function. See\n  `crates/actor-x1/benches/goalzc-crit.rs`.\n\n## Why a trait\n\n- `pub trait Runtime<S>` exists so consumers reach through\n  it (`use ...::Runtime;`) rather than the concrete type.\n  The post-`0.6.0` ActorManager-driven graceful-shutdown\n  work has a stable seam to drop into without rewriting\n  consumer call sites.\n- The trait is object-safe: `&self` / `&mut self`, no\n  generic params on methods. A future `dyn Runtime<S>`\n  consumer compiles without additional plumbing.\n- Inherent `startup` / `pool` on `RuntimeZC` were dropped\n  in `0.6.0-5` so the trait import is *required*. Without\n  it, the call won't compile — the seam is enforced, not\n  decorative.\n\n## Why `Handle` is a concrete struct, not a trait\n\n- Every runtime in scope today and short-horizon (the\n  zerocopy family) shares the same handle shape: per-actor\n  channel senders + per-actor `JoinHandle<u64>`. A trait\n  here would add an associated type with no consumer\n  needing the flexibility.\n- Per CLAUDE.md: don't design for hypothetical future\n  requirements. If a second handle shape arrives (e.g.,\n  the wrapper-trait probed path), lift `Handle` to an\n  associated `type Handle: HandleApi<S>` on `Runtime`\n  *then*. The diff is small and recoverable.\n\n## Counter shape: per-thread `u64`, no atomics\n\n- Each actor thread owns a local `u64`. The hot path\n  (`actor_loop`) increments the local, no atomic ops, no\n  shared memory writes.\n- Aggregation happens at `stop` (sum over `JoinHandle::Output`)\n  and at `query_count` (signal-and-reply, sum over received\n  values).\n- The bot thinks the cost rationale is the right one for\n  the bench-shaped workload (ping-pong saturates a small\n  number of cores, atomics on the count would dominate the\n  per-message budget once `tprobe` is gone). Confirmed\n  empirically across `0.5.0` (probed) and `0.6.0` (probe-\n  free) — see `chores-02.md` / `chores-03.md` for the\n  measured deltas.\n\n## When to choose `query_count` vs `stop`\n\n- `stop` — terminal. Sends `Shutdown`, joins all threads,\n  returns the aggregate. Consumes the handle. Use for\n  one-shot measurement runs.\n- `query_count` — mid-flight. Sends `QueryCount(reply)` to\n  each actor; each replies with its current local count\n  and keeps running. Does *not* consume the handle. Use\n  when the same actor mesh measures many windows in\n  succession (amortized criterion benches; long-running\n  smoke generators wanting periodic snapshots).\n- Two successive `query_count` reads are monotonic non-\n  decreasing but not equal — actors keep counting between\n  calls.\n- `query_count` is point-in-time, not a stop-the-world\n  snapshot. Actors keep processing messages between the\n  `QueryCount` send and the reply send (the signal is\n  serialized with `User` messages in the per-actor\n  channel). For amortized benchmarking this is exactly\n  what's wanted: the reported count covers a wall-clock\n  window that *contains* the `run(measurement)` window\n  plus a small signal-handling delta.\n\n## Why `reset_count` is explicit (no built-in warmup phase)\n\n- The lifecycle API has no automatic warmup → measurement\n  transition. The caller drives both windows with `run`\n  and inserts `reset_count` at the boundary.\n- This keeps `Handle::run(duration)` semantically simple\n  (just \"sleep `duration`, actors keep going\") and avoids\n  baking a warmup discipline into the API surface that\n  not every consumer wants.\n- The bot thinks the alternative (a `run(warmup, measure)`\n  primitive) would over-specialize the API for the\n  measurement use case at the expense of long-running\n  smoke / interactive uses where there is no warmup phase.\n\n## Why `Drop` is silent (panics swallowed)\n\n- `Drop::drop` must not panic-during-drop (a double-panic\n  aborts the process). The drop path sends `Shutdown` to\n  each actor, joins, and discards both the per-thread\n  counts and any join errors.\n- Callers who care about actor-panic visibility must call\n  `stop` explicitly — `stop` propagates panics via the\n  unwrap on the join result. The chores-03 entry under\n  `0.6.0-1` notes this contract; `goalzc-crit` relies on\n  it (drops the handle at end-of-bench, silently).\n\n## Best-effort `reset_count` / `query_count` semantics\n\n- Both methods iterate over the handle's senders and send\n  their signal best-effort: a closed channel (actor thread\n  already exited) is silently skipped.\n- For `reset_count`, the skipped actor's count is captured\n  by its `JoinHandle::Output` and rolled into `stop`'s\n  total — nothing is lost.\n- For `query_count`, the implementation tracks how many\n  sends succeeded and only `recv`s that many replies, so a\n  dead thread can't deadlock the caller. In practice, the\n  handle is alive (pre-`stop`) so all sends succeed; the\n  defensive shape is for symmetry with `reset_count`.\n\n## Drain invariant\n\n- By the time the handle's drop / `stop` completes, every\n  `PooledMsg` ever in flight has been dropped, so\n  `pool.free_len() == pool.size()`.\n- Every lifecycle test in `runtime_zc.rs` asserts this\n  after the run. It's the single property that says \"the\n  shutdown was clean\" — buffers that didn't make it back\n  to the pool would indicate a leaked message, a wedged\n  thread, or a contract violation in `ActorZC`.\n\n## What's intentionally *not* on the lifecycle surface\n\n- **Per-message latency stats / band-table.** Probing left\n  the runtime in `0.6.0-2`; the legacy probed loop and\n  scaffolding were removed in `0.6.0-6`. Probing re-enters\n  in a separate ladder (post-`0.6.0`) via an actor-wrapper\n  trait or `TProbe::Counter` — neither will live on the\n  `Runtime` trait itself. The bot thinks the right home\n  is a wrapper that consumes an `ActorZC` and exposes the\n  same trait + a probe accessor; the runtime stays\n  unaware.\n- **Per-actor count breakdown.** `stop` returns the\n  aggregate; per-actor counts are not exposed today.\n  Trivially recoverable later (change `stop`'s return to\n  `Vec<u64>` or add a sibling method); not done because\n  no consumer needs it yet.\n- **Typed messages to the runtime.** The lifecycle uses\n  internal `SignalZC` variants only; there is no public\n  channel for callers to push typed control messages.\n  The post-`0.6.0` ActorManager-driven graceful-shutdown\n  work is expected to introduce this.\n\n## History\n\n- `0.6.0-0` — plan marker. `chores-03.md`.\n- `0.6.0-1` — `Handle` + `startup` / `run` / `stop`.\n- `0.6.0-2` — `reset_count`; `goalzc` / `goalzc-crit` move\n  to the lifecycle; probing leaves the runtime.\n- `0.6.0-3` — shared `report` module (side-refactor).\n- `0.6.0-4` — `query_count` + amortized `goalzc-crit`.\n- `0.6.0-5` — `pub trait Runtime<S>` + inherent\n  `startup` / `pool` removed; `ClearProbe → ClearCount`.\n- `0.6.0-6` — legacy `run` / `run_probed` /\n  `actor_loop_probed` removed; module is lifecycle-only.\n- `0.6.0` — close: this notes file + worked example in\n  the module rustdoc.\n"
}
```

```
16	- Explore `linkme` / `inventory` for a benchmark harness on top of
17	  `tprobe` — self-registering benchmarks (no `main()` edits per
18	  benchmark); possibly reusable for actor registration too [[9]]
19	- When a benchmark harness lands (criterion and/or the
20	  linkme/inventory idea at `[9]`), add a CPU-warmup helper to
21	  `tprobe` — probably `tprobe::warmup::cpu_boost(iterations)` or
22	  `cpu_boost(Duration)` — since `calibrate()` no longer warms
23	  the CPU itself. Harnesses that call `calibrate()` directly
24	  (without an app-level dispatch loop) need to prime the CPU to
25	  boost frequency first
26	- Add `--repeat N` flag to goal1/goal2 to run the full
27	  warmup+measurement sequence N times and report cross-run mean
28	  and stdev on `mean min-p99` / `adj mean min-p99`. Built-in
29	  replacement for the `for _ in 1..=30; do goal1 2 ...` shell
30	  loop; gives the empirical repeatability number A/B work
31	  depends on. See [[18]] for floors, technique, and
32	  expected-scale heuristics
33	- Revisit band-table `range` semantics — drop the `+1` so
34	  `first == last → range = 0` (true "no spread" instead of
35	  today's "1 tk / rounds to 0 ns"). `last - first` reads
36	  more naturally and matches what a reader expects "range"
37	  to mean. Pre-existing behavior, not introduced in 0.3.0-3
38	- **Bound `TProbe`'s records-buffer memory.** Each scope
39	  appends a 32-byte `Record` to an unbounded `Vec`; at
40	  `goal1 --inner 10` (~13M scopes/s), 20 s of measurement
41	  buffers 260M records ≈ 8 GB minimum, ~16 GB after
42	  `Vec` power-of-2 over-allocation, which swaps/OOMs on
43	  typical dev boxes. Pre-existing from 0.1.0-2 when the
44	  probe was vendored; becomes hard to miss at long
45	  measurement windows + small `inner`. Options (pick one
46	  once we instrument drain cost):
47	  - (a) **Hard cap + truncation counter.** Fixed max
48	    records (e.g. 64M ≈ 2 GB); past the cap, increment a
49	    dropped counter and skip the `push`. Report shows
50	    `truncated: N scopes dropped`. Preserves hot-path
51	    cost. Partial data on very long runs.
52	  - (b) **Periodic inline drain.** When records hit a
53	    threshold, drain into the histogram and continue.
54	    Memory bounded. Adds occasional drain stalls (ms-ish)
55	    that show up in the `p99-max` tail.
56	  - (c) **Inline drain, no buffer.** `end` / `end_batch`
57	    compute `per_event` and call `hist.record` directly.
58	    Fully bounded memory, no stalls, but adds ~15–20 ns
59	    to the hot path — non-trivial when the probe is
60	    measuring ~5 ns work per event.
61	  - (d) **Skinnier record (just `u64` per-event ticks).**
62	    4× memory reduction. Buys time but doesn't fix the
63	    unbounded-growth shape.
64	- Clean up `SingleThreadRuntime::run_for` inner loop:
65	  (a) remove the unnecessary `let Self { actors, queue, probe } = self;`
66	      destructure — disjoint `&mut` through named field paths
67	      (`self.actors[i]`, `self.queue`, `self.probe`) compiles
68	      without the split;
69	  (b) replace `while done < inner { …; done += 1; }` with
70	      `for _ in 0..inner { …; done += 1; }` (break on empty
71	      queue stays) — more idiomatic for a known upper bound,
72	      same generated code under `--release`;
73	  (c) update the struct doc comment that references
74	      "field-split-borrows actors and queue".
```

```
600	    ActorLoopFn<S, R>` parameter; hardcodes the call to

602	    `(Vec<Sender<SignalZC<S>>>, Vec<JoinHandle<u64>>)`
603	    concretely. Dropped the `probe_name_prefix` /

619	    Each exercised the legacy API; all three behaviors

626	### Why drop the `spawn_and_seed` generic instead of keeping it
627	
628	Keeping `spawn_and_seed<R>` "in case probed re-entry needs
629	it" speculatively re-introduces the same function-pointer
630	scaffolding the deletion just removed. Per CLAUDE.md:
631	don't design for hypothetical future requirements. When
632	the wrapper-trait probed path lands, lifting `spawn_and_seed`
633	back to a generic is two minutes of work and the diff is
634	exactly recoverable from this commit's history. Keep
635	present code minimal.
636	
637	### Why drop the legacy tests rather than rewrite them
638	
639	The deleted tests asserted properties of the legacy API
640	(`run_probed` returns nonzero per-actor counts, `run`
641	returns counts, the legacy shutdown path drains the
642	pool). Rewriting them against the lifecycle API would
643	just duplicate the existing `lifecycle_*` tests — the
644	behaviors aren't legacy-specific, they're properties of
645	the actor loop and the drain invariant, which the
646	lifecycle tests already cover. Removing the tests
647	removes redundancy, not coverage.
648	
649	### Smoke validation

656	- The probed re-entry hook described in the chores
657	  ladder is not introduced here — it will land in a
658	  separate ladder post-`0.6.0`.
```
